# S1 startup / D1 decoder fixed shared-load diagnostic

This completed screen contains 4920 raw observations: 4100 retained and 820 warm. Both fresh cohorts are shown separately. VM84 was under foreign shared load; guest server/client CPU labels do not establish independent physical resources. The exact-executable B/T control and all negative cases are retained. There is no pooling, trimming, control normalization, significance or causal attribution.

All timings are milliseconds for the complete fixed work unit, without per-item or per-loop normalization. SQL executor time, whole-command backend CPU/client wall, actual-AM inner CPU/wall, and decoder allocation/materialization/free CPU have separate boundaries. The literal answer ran separately before every measured command and provided identical extra warming. All observations were persisted before parsing/acceptance. Full raw values and same-round pairs remain in the bound analysis files.

S1 and D1 are independent source alternatives over fixed B, with the identical opaque supplied prerequisite. C9 is the existing lazy/gallop/exact-count comparison. This matrix contains no D1+C9 combination and establishes no decoder benefit on C9. CF7352 output sizing is not included.

- Raw B72: `72b9498df9a369e747f8d96efb5a6cd33c2451e2`.
- Fixed B: `8a8c475037bfde101da23cc9b0364c9c020eca93`.
- Canonical S1: `832af7d60b9d4c69a5f922dd1522a917bf337761` (same source tree as historical built commit).
- Canonical D1: `c2b5dd6f9012cc4a27628c9d6c507ac467795ac5` (same source tree as historical built commit).
- C9: `0ab3bd65c8957ea5e25f9ef8981401f0616d99de`.

The original build/source identity receipts remain unchanged. Canonical root-authored commits were published afterwards with identical source trees; that identity correction was not a rebuild.

Pair percentages are `100 * (right / left - 1)` on each same-round retained pair. A negative percentage means the right role had a lower observed metric. Absolute and percentage ranges are minima and maxima over ten retained rounds; stdev is the sample standard deviation.

## Cohort 0

2460 raw / 2050 retained / 410 warm. Raw SHA256 `ebc8bc173098ae210593a5c08fb9556f6e2004cb249f613906fa7b82c9e50c0d`. Literal SHA256 `fab773756323bc8541fa74d1712338b80e812c21568cb8dd6ed06f8bdfa377f2`.

Retained command diagnostics: `{"cpu_ns": 128583592380, "major_faults": 0, "minor_faults": 1, "read_bytes": 0, "runqueue_ns": 7863680170, "write_bytes": 0}`. Queue/fault/I/O observations describe this shared-load run; they do not explain candidate differences.

## Reproduction and limits

Use the accompanying exact build/source manifests, untouched SQL fixture/answers, runtime scripts, SCREEN-REPRODUCE.md and cleanup/source-install receipts. Rebuild and qualify in a new owned namespace on another host; do not relabel new ELFs as these observations. The full analyzer can be replayed locally after restoring raw/literal chunks and preserving relative input paths. All six original builds and correctness gates passed before timing; the main screen stopped all ten accepted clusters, and the separate smoke stopped its five clusters. Root final live inventory/PID proofs provide the external state check.

Startup pays an extra metadata lookup on positive cases; short/inline/OR/NOT and Seq controls remain visible above. Decoder long deltas, tiny lists, segment growth and scalar tails remain visible even if a targeted one-byte case improves. No result here establishes a clean-host speedup, cross-variant causality or production recommendation.

Prior art: [CF7352 output sizing](https://commitfest.postgresql.org/patch/7352/) and [Heikki 2014](https://www.postgresql.org/message-id/52F373CC.4050800%40vmware.com). The retained archive evidence pins these references; no new novelty search is claimed.
